- **2.Kxh3?** Illegal — my rook on h8 guards h3 down the open h-file
- **2.Kg3?** Illegal — my c7 bishop covers g3

- **2.Kg1** — the *only* legal move!

- Kg2/Kh2 — attacked by the h3 bishop
- Kf1/Kxg2-type captures — Bh3 covers everything
- Nothing can capture my knight on e2

Whether White plays **3.Kh1** or **3.Kh2**, mate follows:
- **3.Kh1 Bg2#** — the king can't take (Bf1 guards g2), can't go to h2 (rook's file opens), can't go to g1 (knight covers it)
- **3.Kh2 Bg3+ 4.Kh1 Bg2#** — same mating pattern

**My plan — a forced mate in 2:**
1. **Rook e3→b3+** — The rook is defended by my queen (d1–c2–b3 diagonal), so the king can't capture it. The check can't be blocked (it's adjacent), and no white pawn can capture on b3. White's *only* legal reply is **Ka2**, since:
   - a1, b1, c1, c2 are covered by the queen on d1
   - b1, b3, a3 are covered by the rook
   - b3 is defended by the queen (Kxb3 is illegal)
2. Then **Queen d1→b1#** — checkmate! The queen is protected by the rook on b3 (b-file), and every escape square is covered:
   - a1 (queen), a3 (rook), b2 (queen), b3 (rook/queen), b1 (queen, defended — Kxb1 illegal)

**Tool Call:** `make_move e3b3`
